## Document 007: 10月经济数据点评：内需走弱，增长企稳仍待确认

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月经济数据点评：内需走弱，增长企稳仍待确认.pdf
Report source: 金融街证券
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本公司及作者在自身所知情范围内，与本报告中所评价或推荐的证券不存在法律法规要求披露或采取限制、静默措施的利益冲突。
本报告版权仅为本公司所有，未经书面许可，任何机构和个人不得以任何形式将研报内容和相关信息对外公布、转发、转载、传播、复制、编辑、修改、引用等。如有上述违法行为，本公司保留追究相关方法律责任的权利。
市场有风险，投资需谨慎。投资者不应将本报告作为作出投资决策的唯一参考因素，亦不应认为本报告可以取代自己的判断。在决定投资前，如有需要，投资者务必向专业人士咨询并谨慎决策。
一、投资延续调整，政策支持效果有待进一步释放
1-10月固定资产投资增速-8.1%，其中基础建设投资、制造业投资和房地产开发投资增速分别为-
5.1%、-2.9%和-21.6%，分别拖累全部投资增速1.5、1.0和4.2个百分点。从趋势与季节性特征来看，全
部固投及分项延续 2025 年以来的下行态势，较前九个月进一步下探，当前增速均处于近年季节性低位。
尽管房地产开发投资占固定资产投资的比重已经下降至15.7%，但其仍旧是全部投资下行的核心拖累，贡

献率固投52%的降幅，超过一半；同时制造业投资和基础建设投资呈现顺周期特征，分别贡献了13%和18%
的降幅（剩余17%由其他投资贡献）。
10月当月，全部投资、基建投资、制造业投资和房地产投资增速分别为-16.1%、-13.0%、-9.3%、-
33.25%，较9月降幅分别扩大8.9、7.5、8.4和6.9个百分点，投资增速显著失速。
基建投资的逆周期托底作用未能显现，可能与财政支出偏弱有关。从年内走势看，广义财政支出累
计同比总体偏弱，对基建投资的支撑相对有限。10月，社融口径政府债券净融资为1.18万亿元，剔除当
月用于中央金融机构注资、不直接形成当期实体需求的1500亿元特别国债后，净融资约为1.03万亿，较9
月减少5027亿元。融资端放缓叠加此前财政支出偏弱，可能是10月基建投资降幅进一步扩大的重要原因。
与此同时，民间投资持续走弱，反映投资内生动力仍显不足。10月，民间固定资产投资同比下降
21.1%，降幅较9月扩大8.6个百分点。考虑到民间投资在制造业投资中的占比中枢约为75%-80%，民间部

门投资意愿不足，是制造业投资承压的重要因素。
虽然评估政策力度，还需纳入两本账支出未能完整体现的政策性金融及财政金融协同工具。但此类
工具主要通过补充项目资本金、降低融资成本和分担信用风险支持投资，其效果仍取决于项目收益预期、
配套资金及建设条件。财政支出偏弱可能制约项目配套资金到位，民间投资意愿不足则可能削弱融资支
持的带动效果。在这些约束下，新增融资难以迅速转化为实物工作量，政策支持也就未能充分对冲当期
投资的下行压力。

二、消费延续低增，透支效应与短期放缓交织
10月社会消费品零售总额同比0.37%，较9月放缓0.73个百分点；其中以餐饮、非以旧换新商品和以
旧换新商品零售分别拉动0.08、2.46和-2.17个百分点，较9月变动-0.15、-0.59和0.0个百分点。从结构
看，以旧换新相关商品前期需求集中释放后的透支效应，仍是社零增速偏低的重要拖累；而10月增速进
一步放缓，主要来自餐饮及非以旧换新商品贡献减弱。后者一方面受到烟酒、粮油等基础消费品零售增

速回落的影响，另一方面与金银珠宝类零售在价格回调及去年同期高基数影响下明显放缓有关。拉长时
间看，社零增速中枢仍呈下移态势，消费内生动能有待巩固，需求修复的结构性分化仍在延续。

三、工业生产保持增长，经济企稳基础仍待夯实
从结构看，制造业和公用事业增加值分别增长5.4%和4.4%，仍高于近三年同期均值；采矿业仅增长
0.7%，明显低于同期均值，生产表现偏弱。这一分化或与“反内卷”背景下部分上游行业供给约束加强
有关，也提示上游再通胀与利润改善更多体现为价格修复，尚未有效转化为实际产出扩张动能。
2026年全年经济增长预期目标为4.5%-5%。前三季度，实际GDP累计增长4.6%，以2025年四季度GDP
占全年约27.7%的比重作为近似权重测算，要达到全年4.5%的目标下限，四季度实际GDP增速需达到4.24%，
完成全年增长目标的压力不大。但从10月经济运行的现状分析，如果真的以该目标去完成全年增长，意
味着季度增长水平再度回落，在长期增长中枢下移的背景下，经济能否形成“L形”增长的稳定底部，将

有待进一步确认。
数据来源：iFinD，金融街证券研究所 数据来源：iFinD，金融街证券研究所；灰色为以旧

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧；其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行，通膨風險則偏向上行，因此將保留政策彈性，根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明，強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，台股創高吸引外資匯入，推升台幣最高來到 31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至，台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市，而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局，主要在 6.7000 上方震盪，臨近周末，人行中間價為 6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來新低，不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至 4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔，市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存，美元指數維持高檔震盪，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險，並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在 102 附近盤整，帶領 USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。二、日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢，強調日本已脫離通縮，將完全尊重央行政策獨立性，暗示不反對進一步升息。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪走跌。週初，因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160；週間，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求，加上美債殖利率維持高檔，美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔；臨近週末，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐，後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1100-1.1260

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週邁入新的提存期，央行連日減發存單，令市場資金供
給充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週一央行標售 364 天期定存單 1100 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.602%，低於
市場預期區間 1.61%-1.635%，較上期標售結果下跌 4.1 個基本點。 
週 四 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 受美債利率續創新高影響，週四 10 年期指標券 A15109 成交在 2.05%，創同
年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。 
台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨
警戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，目前油價仍居高難下，通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升，反映需求改善、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性。整體而言，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期，在出口與科技製造業帶動下，
短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險：企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產：外資8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨、Fed政策及企業獲利表現。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高：全球股市(MSCI全球指數)創新高後持有一年，獲得正報酬的機率高達62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高：若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達8.4%，遠高於
連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝：科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。

法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。

日本央行本週料維持利率不變
日本央行利率走勢預估
資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口，受惠於全球企業持續擴建AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼，尤其是積體電路、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁，推升貿易順差走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下，短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業，受惠程度可望高於多數AI新創
公司。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.21

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高：9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元，再創單月新高；年增率高達62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳：10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點49,968點後面臨一波修正，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

## Document 075: 2026年10月金融数据点评：10月新增贷款再现负值，稳投资政策发力推动社融同比多增

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月金融数据点评：10月新增贷款再现负值，稳投资政策发力推动社融同比多增.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
10月新增贷款再现负值，稳投资政策发力推动社融同比多增
——2026年 10月金融数据点评
王青   冯琳
事件：2026年 11月 13日，央行公布的数据显示，2026年 10月新增人民币贷款-1000亿，同比多减
3200亿；10月新增社会融资规模为11429亿，同比多增3251亿；10月末，广义货币(M2)同比增长7.1%，
增速较上月末低0.1个百分点；狭义货币(M1)同比增长4.4%，增速较上月末高1.2个百分点。
基本观点：
总的来看，10月贷款继续“减速提质”。当月新增贷款出现负值，主要源于 10月为季初贷款投放小
月，以及居民部门持续去杠杆，企业债券融资对贷款形成替代。不过，当前贷款利率处于历史低位，科技
贷款、普惠小微贷款等金融“五篇大文章”领域贷款增速明显高于整体信贷增速，信贷投放结构持续向新
向优。社融方面，10月新增社融较大规模同比多增，主要原因是当月稳投资政策集中发力，带动政府债券
融资同比大幅多增；同时，8000亿新型政策性金融工具投放完毕，也带动委托贷款同比延续多增。10月末

M2增速下行，主要源于当月新增贷款为负值，拖累存款派生；另外，当月新增政府债券融资规模同比大幅
多增，带动财政存款大幅增加，也会在短期内压低 M2增速。10月末 M1增速出现较大幅度上行，主要受上
年同期基数明显偏低带动。
展望未来，9月 29日央行调整若干结构性货币政策工具，加大对基建投资、科技融资和中小微企业融
资的支持力度，加之出口有望保持 30%左右的高增速，预计年底前实施全面降息降准等总量型货币政策工
具的可能性下降；监管层将主要引导银行等金融机构加大对国民经济重点领域薄弱环节支持力度，推动
“六张网”建设及“五篇大文章”领域贷款高增。我们判断，短期内央行将主要通过对买断式逆回购、MLF
等在内的中期流动性工具开展加量续作，支持政府债券发行和银行配套信贷投放。这是当前货币政策加大
逆周期调节力度的一个重要发力点。预计年底前贷款“减速提质”过程还会延续，政府债券融资规模上升
将推动社融持续同比多增。

### Page 2

2

具体来看： 
一、10月贷款总量表现依然偏弱，主要受票据融资同比大幅多减拖累，同时居民部门降杠杆继续拖
累信贷总量表现；不过，10月企业中长期贷款恢复同比多增，金融“五篇大文章”领域贷款保持高增，贷
款投向结构持续优化。 
10月为传统信贷小月，当月新增人民币贷款-1000亿，环比少增1.18万亿，符合季节性规律（4月新
增贷款为-100亿，7月为-3400亿）；但当月新增贷款同比多减 3200亿，表明信贷总量扩张依然偏弱。受
同比多减影响，10月末各项贷款余额同比增速较上月末放缓0.1个百分点至4.7%，续创有数据记录以来最
低水平。 
从分项来看，10月新增企业贷款-1600亿，同比大幅多减5100亿，总量表现虽弱，但结构边际改善。
随着近期政府债券供给放量、政策性金融工具加速投放，其对配套贷款的撬动作用逐步显现，10月新增企
业中长期贷款 1600亿，同比多增 1300亿；同时，10月新增企业短贷 100亿，同比多增 2000亿。此外，
在短贷替代效应以及去年同期票据冲量造成高基数影响下，10月票据融资同比大幅多减 8123亿元，拖累
当月新增企业贷款规模为负且同比大幅多减。 
尽管 10 月企业中长期贷款恢复同比多增，但与历年同期相比，新增规模仍然偏低。这一方面与经济
新旧动能转换加速推进有关——新动能融资密度相对较低，对信贷资金的依赖度弱于旧动能；另一方面也
反映出当前市场需求偏弱，企业投资扩产意愿不足，中长期贷款需求相应减弱。此外，企业中长期贷款还
受到以下阶段性因素拖累：一是 2020-2023年银行为支持企业纾困显著加大信贷投放力度，其中中长期贷
款占比较高，今年迎来到期高峰；二是企业债券发行利率下行，推动企业融资结构“以债替贷”。 
居民部门方面，10月新增居民贷款-4300亿元，同比多减 696亿元。其中，新增居民短期贷款-2700
亿元，同比小幅少减166亿元；居民中长期贷款再度转为负增，当月新增-1563亿元，同比多减863亿元。
这表明，当前居民部门仍处于降杠杆、修复资产负债表的进程之中，其主要背景是房地产市场持续调整，
同时，居民消费意愿不足、个体经营活动复苏乏力也对居民贷款需求形成抑制。 
总体来看，10月新增信贷总量依然偏弱，但随着经济转型加速推进，融资结构正经历深层次调整，直
接融资重要性上升，信贷增长已难以完整反映融资增长全貌。9月末以来，央行调整一系列结构性货币政
策工具，重点支持“六张网”建设、科技创新和设备更新以及民营中小微企业等领域，体现了适度宽松的

### Page 3

3
货币政策取向。从贷款结构方面数据看，10月末普惠小微贷款余额、不含房地产业的服务业中长期贷款余
额同比分别增长 6.9%和 9.2%；9月末科技贷款、绿色贷款、普惠贷款、养老产业贷款、数字经济产业贷款
同比分别增长12.0%、13.0%、6.5%、16.2%和15.7%，均高于全部贷款增速，信贷投放结构持续向新向优。
二、主要受政府债券融资多增带动，10月社融同比多增。
10月新增社融 1.14万亿元，环比季节性少增 2.41万亿元，同比多增 3251亿元，带动月末社融存量
同比增速较上月末加快 0.1个百分点至 7.2%。
从分项看，尽管投向实体经济的人民币贷款同比大幅多减，10月社融仍实现较大幅度同比多增，主
要由政府债券融资带动——当月新增政府债券规模环比虽受长假因素影响而有所回落，但新增融资规模仍
然高达 1.18万亿，加之去年同期基数走低，同比大幅多增 6983亿元。与此同时，10月表外票据融资同比
少减1208亿元，企业债券融资同比多增946亿元，对社融同比亦形成支撑；其中企业债券融资同比恢复多

增，背后是当月债券发行利率下行提振企业发债意愿。其他分项中，10月委托贷款、外币贷款同比分别多
增 208亿元和 295亿元，其中委托贷款多增主要源于 10月 8000亿新增政策性金融工具投放完毕，部分资
金经委托贷款渠道落地；信托贷款延续负增，当月同比多减 570亿元；企业股票融资同比少增 348亿元。
三、10月末 M2增速下行，主要源于当月新增贷款为负值，拖累存款派生；另外，当月政府债券融资
规模大幅上升，带动财政存款大幅增加，也会在短期内压低 M2增速。当前 M2增速仍然明显高于名义 GDP
增速，显示货币政策保持支持性立场。10月末 M1增速出现较大幅度上行，主要受上年同期基数明显偏低
带动。
10月末 M2 同比增速为 7.1%，较上月末放缓 0.1个百分点。主要原因是当月新增贷款同比为负值，
各项贷款余额增速下行 0.1个百分点至 4.8%，拖累存款派生。另外，10月新增政府债券融资同比大幅多
增，短期内带动财政存款同比多增 6800亿，也会压低当月 M2增速约 0.2个百分点。不过，近期贸易顺差

规模高位上行，叠加人民币持续升值，企业净结汇顺差显著扩大。这意味着10月还会有较大规模的不计入
M2的企业外币资产，转化为纳入 M2统计的企业存款和居民存款，会阶段性对 M2增速形成重要支撑。总体
来看，当前 M2、存量社融增速运行在 7.1%-7.2%区间，仍显著高于上半年 5.6%的名义 GDP增速，表明当前
社会融资条件处在较为宽松的状态，货币政策保持支持性立场，当前的关键是提振实体经济融资需求。
10月末 M1同比增速为 4.4%，较上月末大幅上行 1.2个百分点，主要原因是上年同期基数明显偏低。
数据显示，2025年 10月 M1环比为-1.0%，降幅明显大于 2021年以来的均值-0.2%，这会直接推高今年 10
月 M1同比增速。整体上看，当前 M1增速仍然明显偏低，背后主要是近期房地产市场持续调整，叠加内需

### Page 4

4
偏弱背景下，企业和居民的投资、消费活跃度较低。
展望未来，9月 29日央行调整若干结构性货币政策工具，加大对基建投资、科技融资和中小微企业融
资的支持力度，加之出口有望保持 30%左右的高增速，预计年底前实施全面降息降准等总量型货币政策工
具的可能性下降；监管层将主要引导银行等金融机构加大对国民经济重点领域薄弱环节支持力度，推动
“六张网”建设及“五篇大文章”领域贷款高增。我们判断，短期内央行将主要通过对买断式逆回购、MLF
等在内的中期流动性工具开展加量续作，支持政府债券发行和银行配套信贷投放。这是当前货币政策加大
逆周期调节力度的一个重要发力点。预计年底前贷款“减速提质”过程还会延续，政府债券融资规模上升
将推动社融持续同比多增。

## Document 160: 261111_ms_quanta

### Page 1

M
Update
Quanta Computer Inc.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 OpM miss; stay OW
Management said AI demand has remained unchanged in recent
weeks. It reiterates confidence in strong double-digit y/y growth
into 2027. General server demand continues to exceed
expectations YTD, driven by agentic AI.
Platform transition weighs on 4Q shipments; VR200 to support CY27 growth: AI
servers contributed 75–80% of 3Q26 server revenue, with CY26 AI revenue on track
for triple-digit y/y growth and ~65% of Quanta’s total revenue. The platform

transition is expected to drive a q/q decline in 4Q26 NVL72 rack shipments, but
management’s tone suggests a milder decline than our prior forecast of 4K racks (vs.
~6.2K in 3Q26). We therefore raise our 4Q26 NVL72 production forecast 1K, to 5K,
implying 21.9K builds in CY26. Management expects shipments to accelerate in 1Q27
as VR200 ramps, supporting high double-digit AI server revenue growth in CY27.
ASIC servers’ revenue contribution is projected to double, from high single digits in

CY26 to double digits in CY27.
Agentic AI supports general server growth: Management noted that YTD general
server shipments have exceeded expectations, with both shipments and revenue
increasing q/q in 3Q26 and further growth expected in 4Q26, primarily driven by
agentic AI. Management expects steady double-digit y/y growth in both shipments
and revenue in CY27, with deployments broadly balanced between x86 and Arm-
based platforms and comparable margin profiles. While industry-wide component

shortages persist, particularly in memory, CPUs and PCBs, management indicated
that tier-1 CSPs continue to secure sufficient supply.
Lower NB volumes and rising costs pressure margins: Quanta’s NB shipments fell
17% q/q, to 9.6M units in 3Q26, weaker than normal seasonality but above guidance
of a 20% decline. Rising memory costs and lower scale weighed on profitability, and
we expect margin pressure to persist in 4Q26. Management guides for another

double-digit q/q shipment decline in 4Q26, albeit narrower than in 3Q26, implying a
15–20% y/y decline for CY26. We project 4Q26 shipments of 8.3M units (-14% q/q)
and CY26 shipments of 39.4M units (-15% y/y). For CY27, management expects NB
shipments to decline in high single digits to 10% y/y and PC/NB revenue to fall to a
single-digit share of the mix, reflecting AI server racks’ substantially higher ASPs.
Keep price target at NT$385 and stay OW: Our target implies 11.8x CY27e P/E or

11.0x CY28e P/E, vs. its three-year range of 6-18x. Risk-reward looks attractive at  9.1x
CY27e P/E, below the three-year average of 11.7x, with ~5.1% dividend yield (DPS of
NT$15.6).

NB staying ﬂat. We project that operating
proﬁt margin will fall to ~2.5% by 2028 (AI

servers are margin-dilutive) vs. ~4.1% in
2025.

### Page 4

M
Update
4
3Q26 Results Summary
Component cost pressure drove 3Q GM miss:
Revenue was NT$1,115.5bn (+8% q/q, +125% y/y), but GM deteriorated to 4.8% (-30bps q/
q, -210bps y/y), which is 40bps below our estimate and 20bps below the Street.
Management cited the  following reasons for the GM miss:
• PC/NB: cost pressures from inflationary component pricing; diminishing economies
of scale.
• Servers: inflationary component pricing pressured server gross margins, despite a

slight decline in AI server shipments.
However, opex was also higher than expected, mainly owing to higher R&D expenses.
Thus, OP came in at NT$32.2bn (-6% q/q, +75% y/y), 7%  below our estimate and 112%
below the Street.
OpM was 2.9% (-40bps q/q, -80bps y/y). Management guided for 2H OpM to be flat h/h,
so we think 3Q OpM was a miss.
Non-op income was higher than expected because of ~NT$3.9bn in FX gains, so net profit
came in at ~NT$28.4bn (-1% q/q, +73% y/y) for EPS of NT$7.36, 6% above our estimate

and 2% above the Street.
Consignment? Under the buy-and-sell model, high-ASP server rack procurement inflated
revenue but diluted gross margins and to a lesser extent operating margins (owing to
operating leverage) via pass-through component accounting. Management continues to
stress absolute profit expansion and sustained >20% ROIC over margin ratios. To mitigate
balance sheet inventory carrying risk and working capital burden, Quanta will continue to

negotiate for a consignment business model with its CSP customers into CY27.

Estimate Changes
We update our model to factor in the 3Q26 margin miss and updated guidance from
management, which leads to increases of 7%, 13%, and 13% changes to our EPS estimates
for 2026, 2027, and 2028, respectively. We primarily raise our revenue assumptions while
lowering our margin assumptions for 2026-2028.

### Page 6

M
Update
6
Price Target and Valuation Methodology
We keep our price target at NT$385, reflecting the offset between raised 2026-28
earnings estimates and a lower medium-term growth rate: This implies 11.8x CY27e and
11.0x CY28e EPS, in line with the average of Quanta's three-year historical range of 11.7x.
We view this as reasonable because we expect the company to benefit from rising AI
server and EV demand over the long term, which will offset the weakness in the PC

segment and drive mix improvements.
We continue to apply a residual income model to derive our price target. Our key
assumptions include:
• A cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of 1.2, equity premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of
1.5%)
• A 7.0% medium-term growth rate – lowered from 8.5% previously, as we bake in
some conservatism on concerns over a potential slowdown in data center build-
out following the US midterm elections (see our Global Team's view), which could

affect Quanta's server business directly.
• A 3% terminal growth rate
Our bull and bear case scenario values also stay unchanged at $545 and NT$230,
respectively.

Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 169: 261112_gs_inventec

Report source: line報告備份

### Page 2

Earnings revision 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 8% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
up our 2026E revenue estimates by 1%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 9%/8% to reflect product mix with higher contribution from the server business (we 
revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 6/6%). We now expect the GM expansion 
to be slower due to the higher server business contribution that is margin dilutive, thus 
revise down GPM by 0.2/0.2/0.2ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Valuation:  
We reiterate our Neutral rating on Inventec with a new 12m TP of NT$56 (from NT$61), 
based on 14.2x 2027E P/E (unchanged), which is the company’s average P/E for the past 
10 years, and factoring in our earnings revision.

### Page 3

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Inventec is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, servers, 
and consumer electronics. While we like Inventec’s solid position in the AI server ODM 
market, we are Neutral rated as we believe valuation already reflects near-term 
positives. We model Inventec earnings growth in 2026-28E to be driven by the 
commercial PC demand recovery and AI server ramp-up. We model AI servers to 
contribute more in 2026-28E. We see P/E valuation as fair at the current level compared 
to its peers and already reflects the solid growth in 2026-28E. Catalysts for further 
upside/downside would be its customers’ demand on AI servers and general server 
demand outlook.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$56 is based on 14.2x 2027E P/E, which is 
the company’s average P/E for the past 10 years. 
Key upside/downside risks: (1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
(2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and (3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

M
Update
eMemory Technology Inc  | Asia Pacific
Security demand resilient amid
cloud slowdown
Record earnings: 3Q revenue was NT$1.21bn (+10.4% Q/Q, +27.1% Y/Y) and
attributable net income was NT$639mn (+10.2% Q/Q, +31.2% Y/Y), 2.7% and 1.6%
above MSe, respectively. Operating income beat MSe by 7.0%, while margin reached
63.2%, 2.6ppt above MSe. Royalties rose 15.4% Q/Q, with 12-inch royalties up 20.2%,
versus 3.5% opex growth. PUF security revenue grew 213% Y/Y to 14.5% of sales.

EPS was NT$8.56.
Royalty outlook: Management expects growth to accelerate on higher advanced-
node rates, product ramps and PUF adoption. Customers favor fewer, higher-value
flagship designs, with advanced-node licensing and royalty values several times
those of mature nodes. Management provided no quantified 4Q guidance.
Security expansion: PUFrt adoption in Arm Neoverse CSS V3, BMC, SSD and
networking broadens data-center exposure. Penetration, content per chip and node

migration support growth, while PQC adds license value. Some 3nm designs are in
production, with 2nm qualification and initial adoption targeted for 2027. PUF-
integrated 1T Flash remains in foundry development, with no launch date. PUF
accounts for only 1.8% of royalties, leaving upside dependent on conversion to
production.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
eMemory Technology Inc (3529.TWO)
We derive our price target from a residual income model. We assume an 8.6% cost of equity
(2.0% risk-free rate, 6.0% equity risk premium, and 1.1 beta), a payout ratio of 90%, a medium-
term earnings growth rate of 23.0%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.4%. Based on these
assumptions, our RI model implies an equity value of NT$249bn, or NT$3,330 per share.
Risks to Upside

n Faster-than-expected PUF adoption
n Stronger-than-expected advanced-node OTP migration
n Higher-than-expected royalty expansion from wafer value growth
Risks to Downside
n Slower-than-expected adoption by key customers
n Slower-than-expected PQC adoption
n Increasing geopolitical and trade policy risks

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關
條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保證。非經本公司同意，不得
將本報告加以引用或轉載。 
1 
6415 矽力杰( Silergy Corp.)

報告日期：2026/11/16          研究員：王蕎程(Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要: 
 本業獲利持續改善：3Q26 營業利益季增 29.7%，營益率提升
至 13.26%；惟 2Q26 受金融資產評價利益挹注，墊高比較基
期，第三季獲利下滑主要來自業外收益回落，並非本業衰退。 
 AIDC 與車用雙引擎帶動成長：AIDC 營收年增逾 120%，新一
代 SPS 預計 2027 年開始放量；車用受惠 MCU 及高壓大電流
產品導入，帶動單車價值提升，預期 2027 年兩大業務營收均
有望成長逾 50%。 
 製程升級與供貨優勢支撐成長：Gen4 產品持續放量，預期 2027
年底營收占比達 50%，搭 配 12 吋產能布局及 Gen5 新品量產，
有利爭取大型客戶，惟封測產能吃緊及成本上升仍為毛利改善
的觀察重點。

一、3Q26 本業獲利持續改善，預估 4Q26 EPS 2.5 元 
矽力*-KY 為電源管理 IC 設計廠，3Q26 營收 72.59 億元（QoQ 
+20.9%、YoY +52.4%），主要受惠 AIDC、工業及車用需求成長，
以及消費電子客戶提前拉貨。毛利率 47.40%，QoQ +0.4 個百分點，
主要因產品組合改善；營業利益 9.63 億元（QoQ +29.7%），營業
利益率 13.26%，QoQ +0.88 個百分點，本業獲利持續改善。惟 2Q26
受金融資產評價利益 9.59 億元挹注，墊高獲利基期，3Q26 因業外
損益回落至 1.11 億元，稅後淨利降至 9.09 億元（QoQ -42.1%），
EPS 2.34 元，獲利下滑主要來自業外收益減少，並非本業衰退。
展望 4Q26，消費電子需求受第三季提前拉貨及終端市場疲弱影
響，手機、電視等產品出貨轉弱，惟資料中心需求仍維持成長，
預估營收 74.63 億元，毛利率 47.80%，稅後 EPS 2.50 元。

二、產品組合優化，AIDC 與車用為明年主要成長動能 
AIDC 為公司主要成長來源，3Q26 營收占比達 17%，YoY +120%。
隨 AI 伺服器 CPU 及 GPU 功耗提高，主板供電系統需要處理更大
電流，同時降低電力損耗及散熱負擔，帶動高效率電源管理 IC 需
求。其中，新一代 SPS（Smart Power Stage）將功率元件、驅動器
及感測功能整合於單一封裝，可改善高電流供電效率並縮小電路
板空間，目前已進入多家客戶驗證階段，預期 2027 年開始貢獻營
收。公司表示，北美客戶對相關產品的需求較為迫切，預期放量
速度將快於中國客戶；此外，伺服器主板、資料中心通訊設備及
SSD 為 AIDC 主要成長應用，三者合計可占 AI 相關營收約 70%～

三、車用單車價值提升，光通訊與 12 吋製程布局 
車用方面，公司持續增加單一車款的晶片供應種類，除既有電源
管理產品外，高壓、大電流電源 IC、MCU 及電池管理相關產品陸
續導入域控制器、電驅等應用，帶動單車產品價值提升。公司表
示，高階車款單車產品價值預計由 2026 年底的 200～220 美元，提
升至 2027 年的 270～300 美元；海外新客戶近期已開始小批量出
貨，明年貢獻可望增加，惟中國客戶仍為主要營收來源，預期 2027
年車用營收成長逾 50%。光通訊方面，隨 AI 資料中心傳輸速率提
升，光模組對供電效率、功耗及散熱的要求同步提高，帶動高效
率電源轉換、雷射驅動電源及整合式電源管理產品需求。公司已
布局 NPO 及 CPO 相關產品，並進入客戶設計導入階段。

製程方面，公司布局 12 吋晶圓產能，目前的產能可支應至少 20
億美元營收規模。隨 AIDC 及車用需求增加，類比 IC 供應鏈產能
趨緊，部分同業仍以 8 吋製程為主，矽力則因 12 吋產能準備較為
充足，有利爭取重視供貨穩定性的大型客戶。公司表示，Gen4 產
品持續放量，預期 2027 年底營收占比約 50%，Gen5 則預計於 2027
年量產。新製程除改善成本結構外，也能支援 AIDC 對電源轉換效
率、功率密度及產品整合度的要求，有利提升高階產品比重。

供應鏈方面，目前主要瓶頸在封裝測試，受設備供應不足及裝機
速度限制，產品生產週期由原先約 4 個月延長至 9 個月，公司須
提前備妥晶圓等待後段排產，帶動在製品增加。預期 2027 年上半
年封測產能仍難明顯改善，較大幅度的產能紓解可能落在 2027 年
下半年至 2028 年。雖然公司前段晶圓供應相對充足，但後段產能
限制仍可能影響出貨速度，且成本上升未必能完全轉嫁客戶。

四、預估 2026 / 2027 年稅後 EPS 10.87 / 14.72 元 
預估 2026 年營收 255.85 億元（YoY +36.0%），毛利率 47.58%，營
業利益 31.33 億元，稅後 EPS 10.87 元。展望 2027 年，受惠 AIDC
及車用新品放量、Gen4 良率改善與高階產品比重提升，預估營收
319.81 億元（YoY +25.0%），毛利率提升至 50.07%；營業利益 57.83
億元（YoY +84.6%）。考量業外收益回歸正常水準，預估稅後 EPS 
14.72 元（YoY +35.4%）。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一. 公司簡介 
矽力杰股份有限公司（簡稱：矽力 KY；代號：6415）主要從事類
比、數位混和 IC 之研發設計及銷售，產品應用範圍廣泛，主要
應用於 3C 產業、雲端伺服器、工業類、汽車、5G 通訊等相關
領域，包括 LED 照明、平板電腦、筆記型電腦、固態硬碟、視
頻監控、伺服器、數位機上盒、智能 音箱、智慧型手機、多媒體
電視、LED 背光模組、路由器、行動電源、汽車電子、通訊基地
台及智能電錶等，並持續增加各種終端應用，如 TV Panel、手機
快充、醫療領域等。

平板電腦、電池充電管理晶片、直流對直流轉換晶片、過流保護
晶片、靜電防護裝置、LED 背光驅動晶片以及 PMU 等；針對 LED

片，EMI 防護裝置等；智慧電表，則提供包含能源監控管理(Energy 
measurement IC)的系統晶片 System on Chip (SOC) 以及高精度電
流檢測方案。

二. 產業趨勢概況 
市場概況 
(1)半導體市場概況與 IC 設計業 
半導體產業為工業類設備、資訊電腦、網路通訊、消費性及汽車
電子產品應用的關鍵性產業，亦是目前主導性的基礎產業之一，
隨著 3C(電腦 Computer、通訊 Communication、消費 Consumer)產
品的蓬勃發展和工業類產品智慧化和低能耗的需求，產品生命週
期縮短而更加快速，並朝多元化及豐富化發展，隨著產品推陳出
新與成長，均促進未來半導體需求及市場穩定發展。國內半導體
產業鏈發展日益成熟，有完整的上、中、下游分工體系，使得 IC 
設計公司得以專精於從事設計工作，再將 IC 的製造委託給晶圓
代工製作成晶圓半成品，經過前段測試後再交由封裝廠進行切割
及封裝，最後再經專業測試廠做後段測 試後，完成最後產品，也
造就了良好的 IC 設計產業發展環境。 IC 元件一般可分為數位
邏輯 IC 及類比 IC 兩大類。數位 IC 利用 0、1 非連續性方式
傳遞訊號，多用於處理數位資訊如計算、控制、儲存等，強調尺
寸、速度與功耗(Process Driven)。類比 IC 則負責處理來自自然界
的連續性類比訊號，如光、 熱、電、速度、壓力等物理量，並作
為橋接上述各類物理訊息與數位電子系統的媒介，需同時藉由製
程、電路設計和半導體元件物理的相互配合，在晶片效能及成本 
上尋求最適化，由於其決定了數位產品所能呈現的最終呈現品

質，因此更為注重元件的特性(Design Driven)，琢磨於可靠度、穩
定度、功率消耗、能源轉換效率、電壓電流控制能力等

(2)電源管理 IC 
隨著環保與節能議題發酵，節能科技為目前重要發展課題之一，
而類比 IC 中 的電源管理 IC 正扮演著不可或缺的關鍵性角
色，電源管理 IC 主要作為在不同的系統運作狀態下，隨時控制
並保持系統內適當電流與電壓正常供給。而電源供應器的好壞，
將會直接影響電子產品的使用壽命。近年來電源供應器均朝向輕
薄短小、省電、耐用的方向發展。而要產品達到這些目標的重要
關鍵，乃在於如何把電源供應器做到高效率(高效能)、高穩壓率和
低雜訊干擾，這其中所有參與管制功能的 IC 即被稱為「電源控
制、管理類比 IC」，每年市場均保持穩定成長，消費電子、5G 網
路通信、移動互聯領域、雲端伺服器、工業應用都是目前最主要
市場，未來並朝向人工智慧及電動車及車連網方向發展。電源管
理單元(PMU)是一種高度集成的電源管理方案，將傳統分立的若干
類電源管理器件整合在單個的封裝之內，可實現更高的電源轉換
效率、更低功耗，以及更少的元件數。PMU 在有限的體積內集成
了盡可能多的電源通道，根據各個子單元的需求、提供高效率的
供電，或提供低雜訊的乾淨電源，又或者是系統必須的上電時序
控制，成為為系統供電的首選。過去十年智慧型手持式裝置在市
場快速發展，總體來看，終端市場整體還將保持快速成長，在此
大背景下，PMU 的市場需求有望實現較之前市場預期更高的發展
態勢。隨著智慧型手持式產品的大螢幕化、輕薄化，電池的體積
也將會越來越小，在相應的電池容量不變的情況下，要求整機的
續航能力要更強，除了不斷降低晶片功耗改善以外，對電源的智
慧化管理也變得不可或缺，因此未來 PMU 市場還會有不錯發展。

未來展望 
電源管理 IC 是類比 IC 最基礎和重要的產品，近年來電源管理
一直是半導體領域的熱門市場之一，且電源管理 IC 是所有電子
產品必備的關鍵零組件，屬於高功率類比電路技術，主要用途為
提供電子產品穩定且適當電壓的電源，將直接影響電子產品的使
用壽命。隨著全球 3C 產品的功能不斷增加，汽車自動駕駛和工
業自動 化需求，終端應用漸漸走向低耗電、輕薄短小與多功能整
合以及對產品壽命與可靠度要求更高的趨勢，為達到此目的，電
源管理 IC 必須做到使電源系統效率提升、節能及縮小 IC 元件
封裝以減少所佔用之電路板面積等重點。另隨著 CPU 不斷向上 
躍進，對電源的穩定性及電壓的精準度也就更為要求。此外，高
整合度不但能減少零件數量，達到降低系統耗電以及提升系統可
靠度及品質等效益，並可提高生產良率，且降低生產工時，進而

降低成本。而隨著環保與節能的議題發酵，節能科技為目前重要

源將成為電源管理 IC 設計的重要方向。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一、預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.50 元

惠 AIDC、工業及車用需求成長，毛利率 47.40%，營業利益 9.63
億元（QoQ +29.7%）；惟 2Q26 金融資產評價利益墊高比較基期，
3Q26 業外收益回落，稅後淨利 9.09 億元（QoQ -42.1%），EPS 2.34
元。展望 4Q26，消費電子需求轉弱，惟資料中心需求維持成長，
預估營收 74.63 億元（QoQ +2.8%），毛利率 47.80%，稅後淨利 9.76
億元，EPS 2.50 元。

二、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 10.87 元

需求增加，毛利率 47.58%，營業毛利 121.74 億元；營業利益 31.33
億元，營業利益率 12.25%，業外損益 15.70 億元，稅後淨利 42.24
億元，稅後 EPS 10.87 元。

三、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 14.72 元 
展望 2027 年，受惠 AIDC 新一代 SPS 產品導入、車用單車價值提
升，以及 Gen4 製程良率改善與高階產品比重增加，預估全年營收
319.81 億元（YoY +25.0%），營業毛利 160.12 億元，毛利率 50.07%；
營業利益 57.83 億元（YoY +84.6%），營業利益率 18.08%，業外
損益 4.51 億元，稅後淨利 57.34 億元（YoY +35.7%），稅後 EPS 14.72
元。

## Document 244: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

기업분석 2026.10.30 www.daishin.com  
  Results Comment 
삼성전자 
(005930) 
 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

## Document 263: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

## Document 363: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

### Page 3

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

permitting pipeline each year. Lengthy delays can therefore leave

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

### Page 7

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, declined to –0.14% in October
from +0.06% in September.

The Leading Index weakened again in October after steadier
results through August-September. At –0.14% the latest six
month growth rate is back in negative, below-trend territory,
broadly similar to the soft reads around the start of the year.
The shift points to a return to below-trend growth momentum
in the first half of 2027. Westpac expects annual GDP growth
to slow back to 1.4% for calendar 2026, with a further
moderation to a sluggish 1%yr pace in 2027.

The Leading Index growth rate is still a touch firmer than
six months ago, the current pace of –0.14% comparing to a
–0.2% back in April, when the War in Iran was first impacting.
The component detail shows mixed moves over the
intervening period. Four components have started to exert
a bigger drag: total hours worked taking –0.2ppts off the
headline growth rate; with a further 0.15ppt drag coming
from the combined effect of softer commodity prices

(measured in AUD terms); a narrowing yield spread (mainly
reflecting the RBA tightening pushing up short term rates);
and rising job loss fears captured by the Westpac-Melbourne
Institute Unemployment Expectations Index. This has been
more than offset by a reduced (but still negative) drag from
the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Expectations
Index (adding +0.25ppts); and firmer reads on local equity
markets and dwelling approvals (adding +0.17ppts on a

combined basis.
Overall, the component mix shows labour market conditions
and tightening monetary policy have become more prominent
drags on momentum. While the Leading Index signal is not
overly weak the decline is clearly worth monitoring with the
detail suggesting there may be more weakening to come.
Leading Index moves back below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines

variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken
individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

## Document 397: Productivity_ reasons for optimism (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Productivity_ reasons for optimism (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#gdp #australia #employment #housing #rba #productivity
Productivity: reasons for optimism
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
08:37 November 17 2026 Share
The supply-side outlook is improving as capital deepening strengthens, reallocation drags
fade and AI diffusion gathers pace.
The supply-side outlook is looking brighter. A pickup in capital deepening and a fading ‘reallocation’
drag are already evident in the Annual National Accounts, with AI diffusion offering further upside.

This gives us greater conviction that productivity growth can return towards 1% or more over time,

### Page 2

Growth in 2024-25 was a little firmer than first thought
The 2025–26 figures show somewhat firmer economic activity and business investment than previously
estimated, particularly in 2024–25. The economy grew by 2.3% in year-average terms, broadly in line
with the initial quarterly National Accounts estimate of 2.4%. However, growth in 2024–25 was revised
up by 0.3ppts to 1.6%, largely reflecting stronger private business investment. Business investment is

now estimated to have increased by 1.1% in 2024–25, compared with the initial estimate of a 0.2%
decline. The revisions do not materially alter the broader productivity story, but they do suggest that the
economy was growing a little more strongly for the same rate of inflation in 2025.
above the RBA’s assumption and closer to Treasury’s. The recovery illustrates why it is important to
focus on longer-term trends in productivity growth rather than on the latest year or two, which can be

volatile.
Capital deepening is returning. Investment is picking up and, outside mining, market-sector capital
per hour worked is now at a record high, rising a strong 1.5% in 2025–26.
The reallocation headwind is easing. The shift of labour towards non-market industries, which
generally have lower measured productivity levels, has reduced the level of aggregate productivity by
around 1ppt over the past 15 years. With the expansion of the non-market sector plateauing, this

drag looks set to fade.
Productivity growth is about more than capital. Multifactor productivity, which captures how efficiently
businesses combine labour and capital, was the dominant driver of market-sector productivity growth
in 2025–26. Meanwhile, stronger investment in IT, utilities and professional services should provide a
further lift as new capital is put to work.

### Page 3

The investment upswing is broadening
The Annuals also provide a richer picture of the supply side, including investment by industry and the
evolution of the capital stock, details that are not available in the quarterly accounts. These data point
to a notable lift in investment in the market (ex-mining) part of the economy, where investment has
gone from around 10% of nominal GDP in to just over 11.5%. Outside of this, investment has been
softer, particularly in the mining sector.

### Page 4

Within the market sector, investment by the IT industry, increasingly focused on data centres,
has risen to its highest share of nominal GDP since the technology boom of the 1990s. Utilities
investment, including renewables and associated infrastructure, is stronger still, at its highest share
since 1983, while investment by the professional services industry has reached a record high.

### Page 5

Across the non-market sector (including health, education and public administration), investment has
also held at a higher share of the economy, driven by the health and public administration (including
defence) industries.

### Page 6

Investment is translating into capital deepening
The investment upswing is also beginning to translate into capital deepening as industries look to
rebuild capital stocks after a period of soft growth. Outside mining, market-sector capital per hour
worked is at a record high and well above its pre-pandemic level. That is an encouraging
development, particularly given that the sector accounts for 70% of total employment.
There are important divergences beneath the aggregate. Mining continues to experience capital

shallowing, as investment has been insufficient to cover wear and tear and capital requirements
associated with additional workers. By contrast, the capital stock in the non-market sector appears to
have caught up with the surge in employment we have seen since the pandemic, with capital per
worker increasing for the first time since the pandemic.

### Page 7

A major productivity headwind is starting to fade
Capital deepening will help support productivity growth across the economy. The Annuals show that
productivity in the market sector (excluding mining) continues to grow by around 1% in year-average
terms, while mining remains a drag on aggregate productivity (down 0.8% in 2025–26 and almost 30%
since 2017).

### Page 8

At the same time, a major recent headwind is easing as the rapid expansion of the non-market sector
approaches slows. This matters for aggregate productivity. Productivity growth reflects both gains
within industries, through capital deepening, technology and better processes, and the ‘reallocation’ of
resources between industries with different productivity levels.
Measured productivity in the non-market sector is considerably lower than across much of the market

economy, partly reflecting measurement issues (output in these sectors is generally not well measured,
particularly when there are quality changes). As employment shifted towards these industries, this
compositional effect weighed on aggregate productivity. Our estimates suggest it has reduced the
level of aggregate productivity by a significant 1ppt over the past 15 years and almost 0.3ppts
over the three years to 2025–26.

### Page 9

Productivity gains were about more than capital
It wasn’t only capital deepening that drove productivity growth. Multifactor productivity (MFP), which
captures how efficiently businesses combine labour and capital through changes in processes,
technology, know-how, was the dominant driver of market-sector productivity growth this year. This
gives us more conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will improve from
its recent lows.

### Page 10

Where productivity is improving, and where it isn’t
At the industry level, some of those recording stronger investment are also beginning to deliver
stronger productivity outcomes, although the picture remains uneven.
IT stands out at the positive end of the spectrum, alongside improving outcomes in manufacturing,
administrative and support services, and transport.. These are also industries where the diffusion of
AI and other digital technologies could deliver further productivity gains. Utilities and

professional services should also benefit as stronger investment translates into greater capital intensity
and efficiency.
At the other end, mining remains a major drag, consistent with the capital shallowing evident in the
investment and capital-stock data. Construction is another notable laggard, with productivity continuing
to deteriorate despite strong demand from housing, population growth, renewable-energy infrastructure
and data-centre projects. Construction productivity fell around 2% in 2025–26 and almost 21% over the

13 years from 2012–13 to 2025–26.

### Page 11

A more constructive supply-side story
The Annuals provide a more constructive supply-side picture than the aggregate productivity figures
alone suggest. Market-sector productivity outside mining continues to advance, the reallocation drag
from the expanding non-market sector looks likely to fade, and capital deepening is strengthening
across important parts of the economy. The strong MFP outcome in the market sector gives us more
conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will improve going ahead.

The critical question is whether the strong investment underway in technology, professional services,
electricity and related infrastructure translates into stronger productivity growth as that new capital is
put to work. If it does, the improvement in capital intensity evident in the Annuals could mark the
beginning of a more durable lift in Australia’s productivity performance.

### Page 12

Related articles

groupresc@dbs.com 
• The uptick in Thai GDP growth to 2.9% yoy in 3Q26 from 
2.4% yoy in 2Q masked a widening K-shaped divergence 
in growth. 
• The strong technology-led investment and export cycle 
will continue at a sustainable pace in 2027. 
• Private consumption will stay constrained by high debt, 
a weak labour market, and muted sentiment. 
• Fiscal space to sustain repeated stimulus measures and 
support household spending is narrowing.  
• Soft tourism is set for a seasonal recovery in 4Q26 and 
a gradual improvement in 2027. 
• We expect the BOT to remain on an extended pause 
throughout 2027, supporting an uneven economy. 
• Forecast implications: We expect real GDP growth and 
headline inflation to ease to 2.0% and 1.0%, 
respectively, in 2027, from revised forecasts of 2.4% 
and 2.1% in 2026 (2.1% and 1.7% previously).

### Page 2

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
2

Real GDP growth picked up to 2.9% 
yoy in 3Q26 from an upwardly revised 
2.4% in 2Q. However, the K-shaped 
divergence in growth widened. We 
forecast growth to ease to 2.0% in 
2027 from an adjusted 2.4% in 2026.

Net trade contributed positively in 3Q, 
as export expansion outpaced robust 
import growth, driven by 
supercharged demand for artificial 
intelligence (AI)-related electronics.

Overall growth gained momentum in 
3Q, but domestic demand was highly 
uneven. Investment surged, while 
private and government consumption 
were sluggish, although household 
spending edged up.

Higher GDP growth in 3Q was notably 
driven by stronger technology-
related industrial expansion, while 
the agriculture and services 
sustained their yoy growth.

### Page 3

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
3

Overall investment growth 
accelerated to 11.8% yoy in 3Q26, the 
strongest pace since 4Q12, with the 
private sector set to remain the 
primary driver.

Monthly data showed a sizeable jump 
in machinery and equipment 
investment in September. While this 
increase is unlikely to be sustained, 
we expect it to stay robust given the 
strong investment project pipeline.

Surging investment has been led 
notably by machinery and equipment 
outlays, underpinned by rising capital 
goods imports and local machinery 
sales relating to electronics.

Strong investment applications in the 
first three quarters of 2026 were 
driven mainly by the digital sector, 
which doubled from a year earlier, 
supported by favourable policies, and 
robust foreign investor interest.

INVESTMENT

4

Electronics exports momentum 
across key ASEAN economies is 
strong heading into 4Q26 and early 
2027, supported by global AI 
tailwinds and the AI infrastructure 
build-out by major hyperscalers.

The US remains a major driver of 
Thailand’s strong goods export 
performance, with exports growing 
for the 27th consecutive month as of 
September 2026.

Thai telecommunications equipment 
exports are benefiting from the 
global AI boom, alongside other 
growing tech shipments. Electronics 
exports expansion should continue at 
a sustainable rate in 2027.

Thailand’s business sentiment 
remains positive, primarily supported 
by strong order books amid robust 
external demand, despite challenges 
including rising costs.

GOODS EXPORTS

### Page 5

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
5

Private consumption remains 
sluggish, reflecting structural 
constraints from elevated household 
debt and a weak labour market, while 
any improvements has been 
temporarily boosted by stimulus.

Foreign tourism has faced a difficult 
2026, but should recover gradually 
into the seasonally strong year-end. 
Visitor arrivals will improve in 2027 as 
airfares fall alongside policy support.

The uptick in consumer confidence 
has stalled, as households contend 
with high living costs, uneven 
economic growth, and flooding. 
Fiscal space for continued 
government support is narrowing.

Visitor arrivals from most markets fell 
in the first 10 months of 2026, with 
ASEAN recording the weakest 
performance. While Chinese visitors 
rose, the recovery remains weak.

PRIVATE CONSUMPTION AND TOURISM

### Page 6

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
6

Global food and oil prices remain 
above year-ago levels, amid supply-
side shocks from adverse El Niño 
weather conditions, and the 
unresolved conflict in the Middle East.

Headline inflation surged to 3.9% yoy 
in October 2026, the highest in 45 
months, driven by faster energy and 
food price increases. Above-target 
inflation will persist into early 2027 
before supply-side pressures fade.

Thailand’s upstream producer prices 
are facing substantial upward 
pressures, which is feeding through 
to consumer prices, but the extent is 
limited by muted consumer demand.

Thailand’s monetary policy is highly 
accommodative, with real interest 
rates falling despite a stable policy 
rate. We expect an extended policy 
rate pause at 1.00% in 2027 to 
support the uneven economy.

INFLATION AND MONETARY POLICY

### Page 7

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
7

Thailand’s current account was 
volatile and negative in the first 10 
months of 2026. Goods trade had a 
sizeable shortfall, and the services 
and income balances were in deficit.

External buffers will remain robust, 
despite falling import cover. This was 
driven mainly by surging imports, 
while foreign reserves have stayed 
resilient despite curbing FX volatility.

The decline in the goods trade 
balance reflected more than higher 
energy prices. It was also driven by 
higher capital goods imports from the 
technology-driven investment boom.

Portfolio flows have weakened and 
are volatile in 2H26 vs. strong inflows 
in 1H. Debt flows would be supported 
by stable domestic monetary policy, 
while equity flows stay volatile.

## Document 438: US Economics Analyst_ 2027 US Outlook_ The End of the Inflation Shocks

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Economics Analyst_ 2027 US Outlook_ The End of the Inflation Shocks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The main economic surprise of 2026, the energy shock from the Iran war, has not n
prevented the US economy from growing at a healthy pace but has reversed 
progress on inflation.  The key drivers of growth and inflation in 2026—the war, 
tax cuts, tariffs, and AI price pressures—should all have less impact next year.  
We expect this to leave 2027 GDP growth at an above-consensus 2.4% and the 
labor market stable, while allowing core PCE inflation to fall substantially to 2.4%. 
Growth should follow the same pattern in 2027 as in 2026: solid consumption n
growth and strong business investment growth offset by drags from housing and 
trade.  Consumption should grow a bit over 2%, driven mainly by firmer and more 
evenly balanced real income growth as inflation moderates and the boosts from 
last year’s tax cuts and wealth effects diminish.  AI drives half of our 8% forecast 
for business investment growth but contributes only modestly to measured GDP 
growth because much of the equipment is imported or undercounted in the GDP 
statistics.  Housing investment will likely contract again as poor affordability 
continues to depress demand. 
Near-potential growth should keep the labor market stable in 2027.  This year n
began with fears of a looming AI-driven layoff wave, but the layoff rate instead 
remained historically low, and the labor market settled into a slightly softer 
low-hire, low-fire form of full employment where wage growth has stabilized at a 
subdued pace of about 3.5%.  We expect this equilibrium to last for another year 
before AI displacement begins to have a larger impact in subsequent years. 
While the near-term inflation outlook remains uncertain, we are more confident n
that inflation will fall substantially by late 2027 as the effects of tariffs, the Iran 
war energy shock, and AI price pressures diminish.  We see these three forces as 
the key to the outlook because the economy is not overheated at a 
macroeconomic level or even at an industry level outside of pockets tied to the 
AI boom, and high inflation does not appear to have become entrenched in 
expectations.  As a result, inflation should moderate as long as these forces 
subside and no new shocks emerge. 
The Fed is likely to remain firmly focused on inflation in 2027.  We forecast two n
more 25bp rate hikes in December 2026 and March 2027 to a peak of 
4.25-4.5%, followed by gradual cuts back to 3.25-3.5% starting in November, the 
final meeting of 2027, once inflation has declined meaningfully.  The FOMC will 
also discuss the conclusions of the task forces next year, but we expect any

### Page 2

institutional changes to be limited.  In particular, a large reduction in the size of the
Fed’s balance sheet is unlikely, though a shift in the composition of its assets is
possible.
The most obvious risk to our forecast of sturdy growth and a long-awaited decline in n
inflation is that the inflation shocks of the last two years, which we and most
forecasters did not anticipate, could last longer, or new shocks could arise.  We also
see downside risks to growth from the war, a longer period of higher interest rates,

or earlier and more rapid job displacement from AI.  But our best guess is that there
will be fewer big policy-driven macroeconomic shocks in 2027.

### Page 3

2027 US Outlook: The End of the Inflation Shocks

The main economic surprise of 2026, the energy shock from the Iran war, has not 
prevented the US economy from growing at a healthy pace.  We expect GDP growth of 
2.5% in 2026 Q4/Q4, in line with our forecast from our 2026 Outlook but above both 
consensus expectations at the time and our forecast after the war began.  In contrast, 
the combination of the energy shock and price pressures sparked by the AI investment 
boom are likely to leave core PCE inflation at 3.2% on a Q4/Q4 basis, a disappointment 
to the more optimistic expectation for a large decline that we and consensus held at the 
start of the year.

The key drivers of growth and inflation in 2026—the war, tax cuts, tariffs, and AI price 
pressures—should all have less incremental impact in 2027.  We expect this to leave 
2027 Q4/Q4 GDP growth at 2.4% (above consensus at 2.2%) and the labor market 
stable, while allowing core PCE inflation to fall to 2.4% (in line with consensus).

### Page 4

The Growth Outlook: Solid Consumption, Strong Business Investment, 
and Weak Housing Balance Out to Near-Potential GDP Growth 
Growth should follow the same pattern in 2027 as in 2026: solid consumption growth 
and strong business investment growth offset by drags from housing and trade.  We 
expect this to balance out to overall GDP growth that is a hair above our 2.3% estimate 
of potential growth.  Relative to consensus, our growth forecast is a touch higher on 
consumer spending, much higher on business investment, and notably weaker on 
housing and net exports.

### Page 5

The resilience of the US economy this year in the face of the energy shock has been 
particularly impressive in the case of consumer spending.  Despite a boost to headline 
inflation that has left real wage growth close to zero this year, consumer spending has 
grown at a strong pace, supported by tax refunds, a positive wealth effect from the 
stock market, and a modest decline in the saving rate. 
Our consumer outlook for 2027 is less complicated: we expect 2.2% growth in consumer 
spending driven mainly by stronger real labor income growth as inflation moderates, 
with special factors playing a more limited role.  We do not expect a meaningful change 
in the saving rate, which is likely close to 5% after accounting for revised spending and 
income data that are incorporated with a lag, putting it roughly in line with what one 
would expect at a time when household wealth is high and layoff risk is low. 
Both real income and consumer spending should grow more evenly across the income 
distribution in 2027.  While we noted last year that real income was actually more in line 
with pre-pandemic trends across income quintiles than the K-shaped narrative 
suggested, in 2026 income growth has been weaker for low-income families, due in part 
to fiscal policy changes and the unequal burden of the energy shock.  Income growth 
should even out in 2027, with a boost at the bottom from lower inflation, cost-of-living 
adjustments to government transfer payments, and the likely reversal of recent cuts to 
social spending.

AI will remain the dominant driver of business investment growth in 2027.  We estimate 
that spending on AI-related hardware and intellectual property will exceed $900bn 
annualized by end-2026 (a $550bn increase from 2025) and $1.4tn by end-2027 (a 
further $500bn increase), roughly 4% of GDP. 
The midterm election results should pose only modest risks to AI investment in 2027.  
We expect newly elected Democratic governors in states that account for over a third of 
planned US data center capacity to impose moratoria on new data centers.  But these 
are unlikely to be long-lived and should only affect projects not yet permitted, leaving 
the vast majority of planned 2027 construction unaffected.

### Page 6

The impact of AI on the official economic statistics will be smaller than company 
spending data suggest, for a few reasons.  First, spending will rise less in real than in 
nominal terms because technology goods prices are likely to rise further.  Second, 
semiconductor investment is undercounted in the business investment statistics.  Third, 
most of the technology goods used for AI are imported and are therefore netted out in 
the GDP calculation. 
Despite the undercounting, AI drives half of our 8% forecast for business investment 
growth in 2027.  Its impact on GDP growth is more nuanced: we estimate it will 
contribute 0.2pp to officially measured GDP growth; 0.7pp to true GDP growth, adding 
back undercounted semiconductor investment and exports of chip design services; or 
0.6pp if we also include positive and negative spillovers such as wealth effects and 
limited crowding out of other investment.  One implication of these calculations is that 
growth in the rest of the economy—GDP growth minus the 0.2pp measured impact—is 
not as weak as often claimed.

### Page 7

Housing is likely to remain the weakest part of the economy in 2027 as very poor 
affordability continues to depress demand.  Single-family homebuilding has remained 
close to pre-pandemic levels so far even though mortgage rates are now 3.5pp higher.  
The main reason has been the housing shortage, which has meant that despite worse 
affordability, demand has remained sufficient to absorb the limited supply available 
without a large decline in home prices, which has sustained the incentive to build.  But 
with the housing shortage now less acute and margins for homebuilders normalizing, 
homebuilding is likely to become more sensitive to changes in interest rates.  Higher 
interest rates will also continue to dampen housing turnover by locking in homeowners 
who have low interest rates on their mortgages.

### Page 8

The Labor Market Outlook: A Slightly Softer Form of Full Employment 
While 2026 began with fears of a looming AI-driven layoff wave, jobless claims instead 
fell even further and the layoff rate remained historically low.  Coupled with a slow pace 
of hiring but a stabilization in job openings at a healthy level, this has left the labor 
market in a slightly softer low-hire, low-fire form of full employment.

Our slack tracker—a composite of ten measures of labor market tightness—likewise 
signals that the labor market is a touch softer than the unemployment rate alone 
suggests.  We expect the current equilibrium to persist in 2027, with job growth 
remaining healthy as the economy grows near potential. 
But companies will eventually want to use AI to reduce labor costs more meaningfully, 
and we expect faster displacement of workers from AI-exposed jobs to gradually push 
the unemployment rate higher in subsequent years.  The early evidence on how 
disruptive this will be is mixed.  The labor market already looks tougher for workers 
displaced from AI-exposed jobs, many of whom are moving into roles with similar or 
higher AI exposure that leave them vulnerable.  But young people, who have tended to 
adapt better historically, are already adjusting by shifting to college majors less exposed 
to AI displacement.

### Page 9

The convergence of our wage growth tracker and survey-based wage growth 
expectations suggests that wage growth is stabilizing at about 3.5%.  With productivity 
growth running above 2% this cycle, this implies subdued inflationary pressure from 
labor costs.  We see little reason to expect wage growth to accelerate meaningfully next 
year because the labor market is not unusually tight and workers appear to be aware 
that finding a new job is harder than usual and concerned about displacement risk from 
AI.
